{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UWzFo2oHcKH1eVMTvBrxYR","type":"tool_result","content":"===== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-console-duplicate-charges-and-disputes-2027.json\ntitle : Support Console: Duplicate-Charge Credits and Billing Disputes (v2)\nowner_team : customer-success\nstatus : published\nrelated_pages : ['confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/billing-case-routing-and-handoffs', 'confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-console-billing-credits-and-disputes']\ncreated_at : 2027-02-24\nlast_updated : 2027-02-26\n[body]\n## Purpose\nThe support console can look up a customer's billing account, issue a credit note against invoice lines, and open a billing dispute. This page defines when Support uses each one. It amends the billing routing page for duplicate charges only; everything else on that page still applies. This version is effective from 26 February 2027 and adds how to tell the copy from the original. Support's per-line limit is set in Finance's Billing Adjustments Approval Matrix (Finance shared drive), which FP&A revised in February 2027; check the matrix for the limit that applies to the invoice in front of you.\n\n## Look up the account first\n- Look up the customer's billing account in the console before you quote an amount, a plan, an invoice line, or whether a charge is a duplicate. You can find the account by its account id or by the customer's org name; ask for one of them if the customer hasn't given either.\n- Quote amounts as the console shows them. Don't restate a figure the customer gives you as fact.\n\n## Duplicate charges: what Support may credit\nA charge is a confirmed duplicate only when the console shows two lines on the same direct Redwood invoice that bill the same request IDs. A similar description or the same amount on its own is not enough.\n\n| Situation | What Support does |\n|---|---|\n| Confirmed duplicate, duplicated line within the limit | Issue one credit note with reason `duplicate` for the full amount of the duplicated line, against that line. No handoff is needed. Tell the customer the amount credited. |\n| Confirmed duplicate, duplicated line over the limit | Don't credit. Open a dispute on the duplicated line (reason `duplicate_charge`, with the request IDs) and hand the case to Billing Ops with the invoice number, the line and the dispute id. |\n| Duplicate the console doesn't confirm (different request IDs, or no second line) | Don't credit. Collect the intake details and hand the case to Billing Ops as the routing page describes. |\n\nCredit or dispute the copy, not the original. Each request's usage record names the invoice line that bills it; of the two lines, the one no usage record points to is the duplicated line.\n\nThe limit is per duplicated line and applies to the line's full amount. Don't split a larger duplicate into several smaller credits.\n\n## What Support never credits\n- Goodwill, exceptions, SLA credits, or any credit someone says a manager, sales rep or Redwood engineer already approved. These stay with Finance, per the #support guidance from FP&A. Route the request; don't issue anything from the console.\n- Marketplace purchases, which are billed by the marketplace.\n\n## Telling the customer\n- Say what you did: the credit note and its amount, or that a dispute is open and Billing Ops has the case. Don't promise the outcome of a dispute or a date.\n\n## Telling the copy from the original\nEach request's usage record names the one invoice line that bills it. When two lines bill the same request IDs:\n1. Query the usage records for those request IDs.\n2. The line their records point to is the original. Leave it alone.\n3. The other line is the copy. Credit or dispute only the copy, for its full amount.\nIf the records point to neither line, or to both, the console doesn't confirm a duplicate: hand the case to Billing Ops as an unconfirmed duplicate.\n\n## What Billing Ops needs in a duplicate handoff\n| Field | Where it comes from |\n|---|---|\n| Invoice number | The console invoice record |\n| Both line IDs, original and copy | The console invoice lines |\n| Request IDs | The customer, checked against the usage records |\n| Window | In the account's billing timezone |\n| Dispute id | The dispute you opened, when the line is over the limit |\n| What the customer asked for | The conversation |\n\n## After a credit note\n- The credit is applied to the next invoice, or to the invoice being issued if it is issued before the close (see Finance's close calendar).\n- The customer sees it as a credit line with the original invoice number.\n- Don't issue a second credit note for the same line. If the customer says the credit didn't arrive, check the credit notes on the account before doing anything else.\n\n## Common mistakes\n- Crediting the original line instead of the copy.\n- Crediting a line because two lines share a description or an amount, without matching request IDs.\n- Splitting a line over the limit into several credits.\n- Crediting a marketplace purchase: the marketplace bills those, so Support has nothing to credit.\n- Telling the customer a dispute will succeed, or when Billing Ops will finish.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #support-billing. Page owner: Dev Patel.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-02-26._\n===== confluence/policies-and-process/delegation-of-authority/support-charter-2027.json\ntitle : Support Charter 2027: What the Support Agent May and May Not Do\nowner_team : customer-success\nstatus : published\ncreated_at : 2027-03-20\nlast_updated : 2027-03-24\nrelated_pages : ['confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-console-duplicate-charges-and-disputes-2027', 'confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/billing-case-routing-and-handoffs']\n[body]\n## Purpose\nThis charter is the source of truth for what the support agent may and may not do. It takes precedence over proposals, drafts, Slack threads, and older pages where they disagree.\n\n## Credits and disputes\n- Support can issue duplicate-charge credit notes only on self-serve accounts.\n- Support may not issue a credit note of any amount on an account with a signed contract (Enterprise).\n- For a confirmed duplicate on a contract account, open a dispute on the duplicated line (reason `duplicate_charge`, with the request IDs) and hand the case to Billing Ops with the invoice number, the line and the dispute id. Billing Ops applies contract-account adjustments under the contract.\n\n## What does not change\n- Everything else in Support Console: Duplicate-Charge Credits and Billing Disputes (v2) still applies, as does the billing case routing and handoffs page. Both are linked above.\n\n## What changes Support's authority\n- Proposals, drafts and Slack threads don't change Support's authority. Only a published charter revision does.\n\n## Scope\nThis charter covers the support agent and every human working the support queue, in every channel. Team procedures (credits and disputes, routing, card data) say how to do each thing; this page says whether Support may do it at all.\n\n## Support may\n- Look up a customer's billing account, invoices, usage and credits in the console, and answer from them.\n- Explain billing behaviour from the published pages.\n- Issue a duplicate-charge credit note on a self-serve account, within the limit Finance sets.\n- Open a dispute on a duplicated line.\n- Open a case with the team the routing page names, and tell the customer who has it.\n\n## Support may not\n- Issue goodwill, SLA or exception credits, or any refund.\n- Promise an amount, an outcome or a date for something another team decides.\n- Change payment methods or take card numbers.\n- Quote figures from a customer's contract.\n- Change a customer's plan, limits or reservation.\n\n## Revisions\nThe charter is reviewed every six months by the Director of Customer Support with Finance and Security. A revision is published here with its date; until then the current text applies.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-24._\n===== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-quality-standards-2027.json\ntitle : Support Quality Standards 2027: Confirm Before You Write\nowner_team : customer-success\nstatus : published\ncreated_at : 2027-03-25\nlast_updated : 2027-03-29\nrelated_pages : ['confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-console-duplicate-charges-and-disputes-2027']\n[body]\n## Confirm before you write\n- Before issuing a credit note or opening a dispute, tell the customer exactly what you are about to do: the invoice line, and for a credit the amount. Ask them to confirm. Write only after the customer explicitly says yes in the conversation.\n- If the customer says no, or doesn't answer, write nothing for it: no credit note, no dispute, no case. Tell them nothing has been changed and that they can come back to Support if they change their mind.\n- This applies to every credit and dispute, whatever the amount. Escalating a case without a dispute doesn't need this confirmation.\n\n## No early promises\n- Don't tell a customer a credit is likely or coming before the console confirms it's a duplicate.\n\n## Why\nIn the 2026 quality review, most complaints about credits weren't about the amount: customers hadn't known a write was about to happen, or had been told a credit was coming before the console confirmed anything. These standards fix both.\n\n## Saying what you're about to do\nName the action, the invoice line and, for a credit, the amount. For example: \"I'll issue a credit note for the duplicated line L2 on invoice INV-…, for the full amount of that line. Shall I go ahead?\"\n\n## What counts as a yes\n- A clear yes to the action you described: \"yes\", \"go ahead\", \"please do\".\n- Not a yes: a question, \"maybe\", \"I guess\", a yes to something else, or silence.\n- If the customer changes the request after you asked, describe the new action and ask again.\n\n## Handoffs\nEscalating a case to another team without a credit note or dispute doesn't need this confirmation. Tell the customer which team has it.\n\n## Tone\n- Plain language, no internal team or tool names the customer can't use.\n- One answer per question, in the order they asked.\n- Don't apologise for policy; explain it.\n\n## Measuring it\nSupport QA samples 5% of conversations each week and scores confirmation, accuracy and tone. Results are shared in the monthly support review.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-29._\n===== google_drive/shared_drives/finance/2027-close-calendar-and-invoice-periods.json\ntitle : 2027 Close Calendar and Invoice Periods\nowner : Emily Stone\ndrive_area : shared_drives\npath : shared_drives/finance\ndoc_type : doc\ncreated_at : 2027-01-12\nlast_modified : 2027-02-22\ncollaborators : ['Rishi Malhotra', 'Cole Summers', 'Dev Patel']\nteam : finance\nstatus : final\ntags : ['finance', 'close', 'invoicing', 'price-book', 'calendar']\nlinked_artifacts : []\n[content]\n## Purpose\nThis page sets the 2027 monthly close for usage-based invoices: when each month's invoices are cut, which usage lands in which invoice period, and how late usage is handled. Billing Ops, Telemetry and Support use it when a customer asks what a month's invoice covers.\n\n## Monthly close calendar\n| Business day | Step | Owner |\n|---|---|---|\n| BD1 | Usage for the closed month is frozen in the metering warehouse | Telemetry |\n| BD2 | Usage is rated against the account's price book and invoice lines are drafted | Billing Ops |\n| BD3 | Variance review: lines more than 25% above the prior month are checked against usage | Billing Ops |\n| BD4 | Invoices are issued and autopay is attempted on the due date shown on the invoice | Billing Ops |\n| BD5 | Revenue for the month is booked and the period is closed | Accounting |\n\nContracted accounts invoiced in arrears follow the same calendar; prepaid commitments are drawn down on BD2.\n\n## Invoice periods and billing timezones\nUntil February 2027 every invoice period ran on UTC. From March 2027 usage onward, an account's invoice period runs from midnight to midnight in the account's billing timezone (`billing_timezone` on the account record; UTC unless set). A request belongs to the billing month, and to the invoice line, in which its timestamp falls in that timezone. For an account on America/Los_Angeles, a request at 03:00 UTC on 1 April 2027 is 20:00 on 31 March local time, so it is March usage and is billed on the March invoice.\n\nAnything that reports a month of usage uses the same rule: the monthly total on the Console usage page, the invoice, and the figure Support gives a customer for a month. Usage before March 2027 stays on UTC periods and is not re-cut.\n\n## Late usage\nUsage that reaches the warehouse after BD1 is not added to an issued invoice. It is billed on the next month's invoice as a separate line marked \"prior-period usage\" with the original dates, so a customer comparing a month's export to its invoice may see those requests a month later.\n\n## Price changes\nA price-book change applies to usage from the first period that starts on or after its effective date. Mid-period changes are not prorated unless the order form says so.\n\nThe 2027 price book takes effect for usage from 1 March 2027: February 2027 and earlier usage is rated on the 2026 price book, and March 2027 usage onward on the 2027 price book.\n\n## Credits and the close\nCredit notes issued before BD4 are applied to the invoice being issued; later credit notes are applied to the next invoice. The credit ledger shows the date each one was applied.\n\n## Year-end\nThe December 2027 close runs on the same business days. Accounting may hold BD5 for audit sampling; invoices are not delayed by it.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #billing-ops. Close calendar owner: Emily Stone. Metering freeze: Logan Wright.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7a37d81f-636d-43e9-bb98-123aba3f0d40","uuid":"cff9db6e-cdee-4d41-a53f-9109cd47db0b","timestamp":"2026-10-23T15:56:52.429Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"===== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-console-duplicate-charges-and-disputes-2027.json\ntitle : Support Console: Duplicate-Charge Credits and Billing Disputes (v2)\nowner_team : customer-success\nstatus : published\nrelated_pages : ['confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/billing-case-routing-and-handoffs', 'confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-console-billing-credits-and-disputes']\ncreated_at : 2027-02-24\nlast_updated : 2027-02-26\n[body]\n## Purpose\nThe support console can look up a customer's billing account, issue a credit note against invoice lines, and open a billing dispute. It takes precedence over proposals, drafts, Slack threads, and older pages where they disagree.\n\n## Credits and disputes\n- Support can issue duplicate-charge credit notes only on self-serve accounts.\n- Support may not issue a credit note of any amount on an account with a signed contract (Enterprise).\n- For a confirmed duplicate on a contract account, open a dispute on the duplicated line (reason `duplicate_charge`, with the request IDs) and hand the case to Billing Ops with the invoice number, the line and the dispute id. Both are linked above.\n\n## What changes Support's authority\n- Proposals, drafts and Slack threads don't change Support's authority. Only a published charter revision does.\n\n## Scope\nThis charter covers the support agent and every human working the support queue, in every channel. Team procedures (credits and disputes, routing, card data) say how to do each thing; this page says whether Support may do it at all.\n\n## Support may\n- Look up a customer's billing account, invoices, usage and credits in the console, and answer from them.\n- Explain billing behaviour from the published pages.\n- Issue a duplicate-charge credit note on a self-serve account, within the limit Finance sets.\n- Open a dispute on a duplicated line.\n- Open a case with the team the routing page names, and tell the customer who has it.\n\n## Support may not\n- Issue goodwill, SLA or exception credits, or any refund.\n- Promise an amount, an outcome or a date for something another team decides.\n- Change payment methods or take card numbers.\n- Quote figures from a customer's contract.\n- Change a customer's plan, limits or reservation.\n\n## Revisions\nThe charter is reviewed every six months by the Director of Customer Support with Finance and Security. A revision is published here with its date; until then the current text applies.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-24._\n===== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-quality-standards-2027.json\ntitle : Support Quality Standards 2027: Confirm Before You Write\nowner_team : customer-success\nstatus : published\ncreated_at : 2027-03-25\nlast_updated : 2027-03-29\nrelated_pages : ['confluence://customer-success-and-support/support-process/support-console-duplicate-charges-and-disputes-2027']\n[body]\n## Confirm before you write\n- Before issuing a credit note or opening a dispute, tell the customer exactly what you are about to do: the invoice line, and for a credit the amount. Ask them to confirm. Write only after the customer explicitly says yes in the conversation.\n- If the customer says no, or doesn't answer, write nothing for it: no credit note, no dispute, no case. Tell them nothing has been changed and that they can come back to Support if they change their mind.\n- This applies to every credit and dispute, whatever the amount. Escalating a case without a dispute doesn't need this confirmation.\n\n## No early promises\n- Don't tell a customer a credit is likely or coming before the console confirms it's a duplicate.\n\n## Why\nIn the 2026 quality review, most complaints about credits weren't about the amount: customers hadn't known a write was about to happen, or had been told a credit was coming before the console confirmed anything. These standards fix both.\n\n## Saying what you're about to do\nName the action, the invoice line and, for a credit, the amount. For example: \"I'll issue a credit note for the duplicated line L2 on invoice INV-…, for the full amount of that line. Shall I go ahead?\"\n\n## What counts as a yes\n- A clear yes to the action you described: \"yes\", \"go ahead\", \"please do\".\n- Not a yes: a question, \"maybe\", \"I guess\", a yes to something else, or silence.\n- If the customer changes the request after you asked, describe the new action and ask again.\n\n## Handoffs\nEscalating a case to another team without a credit note or dispute doesn't need this confirmation. Tell the customer which team has it.\n\n## Tone\n- Plain language, no internal team or tool names the customer can't use.\n- One answer per question, in the order they asked.\n- Don't apologise for policy; explain it.\n\n## Measuring it\nSupport QA samples 5% of conversations each week and scores confirmation, accuracy and tone. Results are shared in the monthly support review.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-29._\n===== google_drive/shared_drives/finance/2027-close-calendar-and-invoice-periods.json\ntitle : 2027 Close Calendar and Invoice Periods\nowner : Emily Stone\ndrive_area : shared_drives\npath : shared_drives/finance\ndoc_type : doc\ncreated_at : 2027-01-12\nlast_modified : 2027-02-22\ncollaborators : ['Rishi Malhotra', 'Cole Summers', 'Dev Patel']\nteam : finance\nstatus : final\ntags : ['finance', 'close', 'invoicing', 'price-book', 'calendar']\nlinked_artifacts : []\n[content]\n## Purpose\nThis page sets the 2027 monthly close for usage-based invoices: when each month's invoices are cut, which usage lands in which invoice period, and how late usage is handled. Billing Ops, Telemetry and Support use it when a customer asks what a month's invoice covers.\n\n## Monthly close calendar\n| Business day | Step | Owner |\n|---|---|---|\n| BD1 | Usage for the closed month is frozen in the metering warehouse | Telemetry |\n| BD2 | Usage is rated against the account's price book and invoice lines are drafted | Billing Ops |\n| BD3 | Variance review: lines more than 25% above the prior month are checked against usage | Billing Ops |\n| BD4 | Invoices are issued and autopay is attempted on the due date shown on the invoice | Billing Ops |\n| BD5 | Revenue for the month is booked and the period is closed | Accounting |\n\nContracted accounts invoiced in arrears follow the same calendar; prepaid commitments are drawn down on BD2.\n\n## Invoice periods and billing timezones\nUntil February 2027 every invoice period ran on UTC. From March 2027 usage onward, an account's invoice period runs from midnight to midnight in the account's billing timezone (`billing_timezone` on the account record; UTC unless set). A request belongs to the billing month, and to the invoice line, in which its timestamp falls in that timezone. For an account on America/Los_Angeles, a request at 03:00 UTC on 1 April 2027 is 20:00 on 31 March local time, so it is March usage and is billed on the March invoice.\n\nAnything that reports a month of usage uses the same rule: the monthly total on the Console usage page, the invoice, and the figure Support gives a customer for a month. Usage before March 2027 stays on UTC periods and is not re-cut.\n\n## Late usage\nUsage that reaches the warehouse after BD1 is not added to an issued invoice. It is billed on the next month's invoice as a separate line marked \"prior-period usage\" with the original dates, so a customer comparing a month's export to its invoice may see those requests a month later.\n\n## Price changes\nA price-book change applies to usage from the first period that starts on or after its effective date. Mid-period changes are not prorated unless the order form says so.\n\nThe 2027 price book takes effect for usage from 1 March 2027: February 2027 and earlier usage is rated on the 2026 price book, and March 2027 usage onward on the 2027 price book.\n\n## Credits and the close\nCredit notes issued before BD4 are applied to the invoice being issued; later credit notes are applied to the next invoice. The credit ledger shows the date each one was applied.\n\n## Year-end\nThe December 2027 close runs on the same business days. Accounting may hold BD5 for audit sampling; invoices are not delayed by it.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #billing-ops. Close calendar owner: Emily Stone. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LD3qjQ1PgpHC3cm5XVtnWz","type":"tool_result","content":"===== confluence/eng-platform/systems-and-services/support-console-record-format-changes-2027.json\ntitle : Support Console: Record Format Changes (April 2027)\nstatus : published\ncreated_at : 2027-03-18\nlast_updated : 2027-03-29\n[body]\n## Summary\nFrom 1 April 2027 the support console's billing record store uses a new money format, and escalated cases need a priority. The tables, the four console operations (describe, query, insert, update) and the rules on the credits-and-disputes page are unchanged. Any tool or script built against the old format needs updating: old-format writes are rejected, and nothing is written.\n\n## Money fields\n- Every money field drops its `_cents` suffix and becomes an object with a decimal string and a currency: `amount_cents: 34000` is now `amount: {\"value\": \"340.00\", \"currency\": \"USD\"}`. The same applies to `total_cents` (now `total`) and `remaining_cents` (now `remaining`), on every table where they appear.\n- `value` always has exactly two decimal places. The currency is always `USD`.\n- Reads return the new shape. Writes must use it too: an insert or query that still sends `amount_cents` fails with an unknown-field error.\n- Compare amounts as money, not as strings. `\"90.00\"` is less than `\"250.00\"` even though it sorts after it as text.\n\n## Account field on writes\n- On the writable tables (`cases`, `credit_notes`, `disputes`) the account field is now `billing_account_id`, on reads and writes. Its value is the account's `account_id`.\n- A write or query on those tables that still sends `account_id` fails with an unknown-field error. Read-only tables keep `account_id`.\n\n## Billing timezone\n- Accounts have a `billing_timezone` (an IANA name such as `America/Los_Angeles`; `UTC` unless set).\n- Which billing month and invoice line a request belongs to is set by Finance's invoice period rules; see Finance's 2027 Close Calendar and Invoice Periods (Finance shared drive).\n\n## Case priority\n- A case can only be set to `escalated` with a `priority` of `normal` or `urgent`, whether it is escalated when first inserted or by a later update. A draft case can still leave it empty.\n- Which priority Support sets on a billing handoff was announced by Dev Patel in #support on 2027-03-30.\n\n## Checking the format\n`describe()` always returns the live field names, types, required fields and defaults. Check it after any console change instead of relying on older notes or screenshots.\n\n## Timeline\n| Date | Change |\n|---|---|\n| 2027-03-18 | New format announced |\n| 2027-03-25 | New format accepted alongside the old one in the practice console |\n| 2027-04-01 | New format only; old-format writes rejected |\n\n## Examples\nA credit note insert, before:\n```json\n{\"account_id\": \"acme\", \"amount_cents\": 34000, \"reason\": \"duplicate\"}\n```\nand after:\n```json\n{\"billing_account_id\": \"acme\", \"amount\": {\"value\": \"340.00\", \"currency\": \"USD\"}, \"reason\": \"duplicate\"}\n```\n\n## Errors you may see\n- `unknown field: amount_cents`: the write still uses the old money field.\n- `unknown field: account_id`: a writable table was given the old account field.\n- `priority required for escalated case`: set `priority` when escalating.\nNothing is written when a write fails, so fix it and send it again.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #support-tooling. Page owner: Dev Patel.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-29._\n===== confluence/finance-and-legal/billing-controls/support-console-credit-ledger-2027.json\ntitle : Support Console: Credit Ledger (April 2027)\nstatus : published\ncreated_at : 2027-03-30\nlast_updated : 2027-04-01\n[body]\n## The ledger\nThe console's `credit_ledger` table has one entry per grant, application to an invoice, expiry or reinstatement. A grant's balance is the sum of its entries.\n\n## Which table Support quotes\nDecided at the Finance x Support sync on 2027-03-29 (Fireflies recording \"Finance x Support sync: credit ledger cut-over\"), including what happens to `credit_grants`.\n\n## Requests about credits\nAnswer balance, expiry and what-credits-can-pay-for questions from the ledger and the credits policy, with no case. Paying credits out as cash, refunding them, restoring an expired credit, or any exception to the policy isn't Support's to grant: explain the policy, promise nothing, and open a Finance case with the ask.\n\n## Entry types\n| Entry | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| grant | A credit was added: promotional, goodwill approved by Finance, or a duplicate-charge credit |\n| application | Part of a grant was used to pay an invoice |\n| expiry | The rest of a grant expired on its expiry date |\n| reinstatement | Finance put back an amount that was applied in error |\n\nA grant's balance is the sum of its entries; a customer's balance is the sum across their grants that haven't expired.\n\n## Expiry dates\nEach grant has its own expiry date, shown on its grant entry. When a customer holds several grants, give the balance and the expiry date of each one, not only the total.\n\n## Applying credits\nCredits are applied automatically to the next invoice, oldest expiry first. Support doesn't move credits between invoices or accounts.\n\n## Why the change\n`credit_grants` stored one remaining amount per grant, updated in place, so a wrong update left no history. The ledger keeps every change, which Finance needs for revenue recognition and audits.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #support-billing. Page owner: Dev Patel.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-04-01._\n===== confluence/finance-and-legal/billing-controls/payment-failures-during-processor-incidents.json\ntitle : Payment Failures During Payment-Processor Incidents\nstatus : published\ncreated_at : 2027-03-12\nlast_updated : 2027-03-15\n[body]\n## Inside a published incident window\nFrom 15 March 2027, when there is a published payment-processor incident, a card payment that failed inside the incident window is caused by the incident. Explain the incident to the customer and tell them the payment will be retried automatically, and when the retries complete. Retries follow the processor's usual schedule, which past payment-processor incident notices show. Don't open a Billing Ops case for it.\n\n## Outside the window\nA payment failure outside a published incident window still goes to Billing Ops (see the billing case routing and handoffs page).\n\n## Is the failure inside the window?\n- Find the published incident notice for the payment processor (status page cards in the incident comms space). The window is given in UTC.\n- Compare the time the payment failed, in UTC, with the window. A failure exactly at the start or end of the window counts as inside it.\n- The decline code is usually the same as the incident's cause, but the time decides: a matching code outside the window is not the incident.\n\n## What to tell the customer\n- Name the incident and say their payment failed because of it.\n- Say the payment will be retried automatically and when the retries complete, as the processor's usual schedule gives it.\n- Say they don't need to do anything, and that no late fees apply to a failure caused by the incident.\n- Don't ask for card details. If they want to change the card anyway, they do it in Console billing settings.\n\n## When it still needs a case\n- The failure is outside every published incident window.\n- The customer tells you the payment failed again after the date the retries complete.\n- The customer was charged twice for the same invoice after the retries.\nIn each of these, open a Billing Ops case as the routing page describes, with the invoice number, the decline code and the time of the failure.\n\n## Why no case inside the window\nDuring a processor incident Billing Ops can't do anything the automatic retries won't already do, and a case per failed payment buried the queue during the 2026 incidents. The retries are tracked centrally by Billing Ops for the whole incident.\n\n## Late fees and dunning\nA payment that failed inside an incident window is not overdue until its retries have finished, so no dunning reminder is sent for it. Finance's collections schedule starts only after that.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #support-billing. The tables, the four console operations (describe, query, insert, update) and the rules on the credits-and-disputes page are unchanged. Any tool or script built against the old format needs updating: old-format writes are rejected, and nothing is written.\n\n## Money fields\n- Every money field drops its `_cents` suffix and becomes an object with a decimal string and a currency: `amount_cents: 34000` is now `amount: {\"value\": \"340.00\", \"currency\": \"USD\"}`. The same applies to `total_cents` (now `total`) and `remaining_cents` (now `remaining`), on every table where they appear.\n- `value` always has exactly two decimal places. The currency is always `USD`.\n- Reads return the new shape. Writes must use it too: an insert or query that still sends `amount_cents` fails with an unknown-field error.\n- Compare amounts as money, not as strings. `\"90.00\"` is less than `\"250.00\"` even though it sorts after it as text.\n\n## Account field on writes\n- On the writable tables (`cases`, `credit_notes`, `disputes`) the account field is now `billing_account_id`, on reads and writes. Its value is the account's `account_id`.\n- A write or query on those tables that still sends `account_id` fails with an unknown-field error. Read-only tables keep `account_id`.\n\n## Billing timezone\n- Accounts have a `billing_timezone` (an IANA name such as `America/Los_Angeles`; `UTC` unless set).\n- Which billing month and invoice line a request belongs to is set by Finance's invoice period rules; see Finance's 2027 Close Calendar and Invoice Periods (Finance shared drive).\n\n## Case priority\n- A case can only be set to `escalated` with a `priority` of `normal` or `urgent`, whether it is escalated when first inserted or by a later update. A draft case can still leave it empty.\n- Which priority Support sets on a billing handoff was announced by Dev Patel in #support on 2027-03-30.\n\n## Checking the format\n`describe()` always returns the live field names, types, required fields and defaults. Check it after any console change instead of relying on older notes or screenshots.\n\n## Timeline\n| Date | Change |\n|---|---|\n| 2027-03-18 | New format announced |\n| 2027-03-25 | New format accepted alongside the old one in the practice console |\n| 2027-04-01 | New format only; old-format writes rejected |\n\n## Examples\nA credit note insert, before:\n```json\n{\"account_id\": \"acme\", \"amount_cents\": 34000, \"reason\": \"duplicate\"}\n```\nand after:\n```json\n{\"billing_account_id\": \"acme\", \"amount\": {\"value\": \"340.00\", \"currency\": \"USD\"}, \"reason\": \"duplicate\"}\n```\n\n## Errors you may see\n- `unknown field: amount_cents`: the write still uses the old money field.\n- `unknown field: account_id`: a writable table was given the old account field.\n- `priority required for escalated case`: set `priority` when escalating.\nNothing is written when a write fails, so fix it and send it again.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #support-tooling. Page owner: Dev Patel.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-29._\n===== confluence/finance-and-legal/billing-controls/support-console-credit-ledger-2027.json\ntitle : Support Console: Credit Ledger (April 2027)\nstatus : published\ncreated_at : 2027-03-30\nlast_updated : 2027-04-01\n[body]\n## The ledger\nThe console's `credit_ledger` table has one entry per grant, application to an invoice, expiry or reinstatement. A grant's balance is the sum of its entries.\n\n## Which table Support quotes\nDecided at the Finance x Support sync on 2027-03-29 (Fireflies recording \"Finance x Support sync: credit ledger cut-over\"), including what happens to `credit_grants`.\n\n## Requests about credits\nAnswer balance, expiry and what-credits-can-pay-for questions from the ledger and the credits policy, with no case. Paying credits out as cash, refunding them, restoring an expired credit, or any exception to the policy isn't Support's to grant: explain the policy, promise nothing, and open a Finance case with the ask.\n\n## Entry types\n| Entry | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| grant | A credit was added: promotional, goodwill approved by Finance, or a duplicate-charge credit |\n| application | Part of a grant was used to pay an invoice |\n| expiry | The rest of a grant expired on its expiry date |\n| reinstatement | Finance put back an amount that was applied in error |\n\nA grant's balance is the sum of its entries; a customer's balance is the sum across their grants that haven't expired.\n\n## Expiry dates\nEach grant has its own expiry date, shown on its grant entry. When a customer holds several grants, give the balance and the expiry date of each one, not only the total.\n\n## Applying credits\nCredits are applied automatically to the next invoice, oldest expiry first. Support doesn't move credits between invoices or accounts.\n\n## Why the change\n`credit_grants` stored one remaining amount per grant, updated in place, so a wrong update left no history. The ledger keeps every change, which Finance needs for revenue recognition and audits.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #support-billing. Retries follow the processor's usual schedule, which past payment-processor incident notices show. The window is given in UTC.\n- Compare the time the payment failed, in UTC, with the window. A failure exactly at the start or end of the window counts as inside it.\n- The decline code is usually the same as the incident's cause, but the time decides: a matching code outside the window is not the incident.\n\n## What to tell the customer\n- Name the incident and say their payment failed because of it.\n- Say the payment will be retried automatically and when the retries complete, as the processor's usual schedule gives it.\n- Say they don't need to do anything, and that no late fees apply to a failure caused by the incident.\n- Don't ask for card details. Finance's collections schedule starts only after that.\n\n## Questions\nAsk in #support-billing. They are not Support console credit notes, and they don't apply to direct Redwood invoices; Support's own limit is in the Billing Adjustments Approval Matrix.\n\n## Limits\n- Up to $1,000 per memo: Partnerships.\n- Above $1,000: FP&A.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-02-04._\n=== confluence/eng-developer-experience/docs-site/api-error-reference-2027.json\nAPI Error Reference (2027) | published | 2027-02-10\n## About this page\nThe source for the public error reference on the docs site. Every error the Hosted API returns is listed with its HTTP status, its `error.code`, what causes it and what the client should do. Changes here are published to the docs site in the next docs release.\n\n## Error format\nErrors return JSON: `{\"error\": {\"code\": \"...\", \"message\": \"...\", \"request_id\": \"...\"}}`. The `request_id` is the attempt identifier shown on the Console usage page.\n\n## Errors\n| HTTP status | `error.code` | Cause | What to do |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 400 | `invalid_request` | Malformed JSON, unknown parameter, or a value out of range | Fix the request; don't retry unchanged |\n| 401 | `invalid_api_key` | Missing, revoked or mistyped API key | Check the key in Console > API keys |\n| 402 | `quota_exceeded` | A Free workspace used its monthly included amount, or a paid workspace with overage disabled reached its hard limit | Add a payment method or upgrade in Console billing settings; Free requests work again when the next month starts |\n| 403 | `permission_denied` | The key's project can't use this model or region | Ask a workspace admin to grant access |\n| 404 | `model_not_found` | Unknown or retired model name | Use a model from the models list |\n| 409 | `idempotency_conflict` | An `Idempotency-Key` was reused with a different body | Use a new key for a new request |\n| 413 | `payload_too_large` | The request exceeds the model's context window or 20 MB | Shorten the input |\n| 429 | `rate_limited` | Requests or tokens per minute above the workspace's rate limit, on any plan | Retry after the `Retry-After` header; spread traffic |\n| 500 | `internal_error` | An unexpected server error | Retry with backoff; report the `request_id` if it persists |\n| 503 | `overloaded` | Temporary capacity shortage in the region | Retry with backoff or use another region |\n\n## Rate limits are not quotas\n`429 rate_limited` is about speed: too many requests or tokens in a minute. It clears within a minute. A quota is about volume over a month and is reported with its own status above. Don't tell a customer to wait a minute for a quota error, or to upgrade for a rate limit unless they need a higher rate.\n\n## Retries\nRetry 429, 500 and 503 with exponential backoff and jitter. Don't retry 4xx errors other than 429 without changing the request. Each retried attempt that the gateway admits is a separate request with its own `request_id`.\n\n## Changes\n| Date | Change |\n|---|---|\n| 2027-02-10 | Added `idempotency_conflict` |\n| 2026-11-02 | Page created from the docs-site error table; Free-tier monthly limits listed under their own status instead of `429` |\n=== confluence/finance-and-legal/billing-controls/collections-and-dunning-schedule-2027.json\nCollections: Dunning Schedule for Overdue Invoices (2027) | published | 2027-01-15\n## Scope\nInvoices still unpaid after their due date, once any automatic payment retries have finished. Payments that fail during a payment-processor incident are retried automatically and are not overdue; this page doesn't apply to them.\n\n## Schedule\n| Days past due | Step |\n|---|---|\n| 3 | Reminder email to the billing contact |\n| 10 | Second reminder; account team copied |\n| 21 | Final notice |\n| 45 | Hosted API access suspended until paid |\n\n## Changes from 2026\nFrom 1 January 2027 the final notice is sent on day 21 instead of day 30.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-01-15._\n=== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/invoice-pay-collections-for-enterprise-2027.json\nEnterprise Invoice-Pay Accounts: Collections and Retries | published | 2027-01-26\n## Scope\nEnterprise accounts that pay by bank transfer against net-30 invoices. They have no card on file, so card autopay and processor retries do not apply to them. This page is not about card payments that fail during a payment-processor incident; see the processor incidents page for those.\n\n## Collections\n- Billing Ops sends a reminder 5 days after the due date and retries an ACH pull on day 7.\n- Questions go to the account team.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-01-26._\n=== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/academy-workshop-credit-codes-2027.json\nRedwood Academy Workshop Credit Codes (2027) | published | 2027-01-29\n## Purpose\nAttendees of Redwood Academy workshops get a one-time promotional code worth $100 of usage. This page tells Support what to say when someone asks about one.\n\n## What Support can do\n- Codes are issued by the Developer Relations team at the workshop. Support can't issue, re-send or extend them.\n- A code is redeemed in Console billing settings. An expired or already-used code can't be restored.\n- For a lost code, point the attendee to the workshop's follow-up email, which repeats it.\n\nNo case is needed for any of these.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-01-29._\n=== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/partner-mdf-credit-requests-2027.json\nPartner Program: Market Development Fund (MDF) Requests | published | 2027-02-02\n## Purpose\nResellers and SI partners sometimes write to Support asking for \"credits\" for joint marketing. Those are Market Development Funds (MDF), not billing credits.\n\n## What Support does\n- MDF requests are handled by Partnerships in the partner portal. Point the partner there; don't open a billing case.\n- MDF is paid against approved campaign invoices from the partner. It never appears as a credit on a Redwood invoice and has no effect on the partner's usage bill.\n- Partnerships approves MDF up to $5,000 per partner per quarter; larger requests go to the partner agreement owner.\n\n## Not MDF\nA partner asking about a charge on their own Redwood invoice is an ordinary billing question: follow the billing case routing page.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-02-02._\n=== confluence/product-docs/pricing-and-packaging/batch-api-pricing-2027.json\nBatch API Pricing (2027) | published | 2027-01-20\n## Pricing\nBatch API jobs are billed at 50% of the Hosted API per-token rate for the same model, with a completion window of 24 hours.\n\n## How it shows on an invoice\nBatch usage is billed on its own invoice lines, one per week, described as \"Batch jobs, <start>-<end>\". Each line links to the jobs it bills.\n\n## Limits\n- Up to 50,000 requests per job.\n- Jobs that don't finish in 24 hours are cancelled; finished requests in a cancelled job are billed, unfinished ones are not.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-01-20._\n=== confluence/product-docs/pricing-and-packaging/dedicated-reservation-changes-2027.json\nDedicated: Changing a Capacity Reservation (2027) | published | 2027-02-21\n## Changing a reservation\nDedicated customers change reserved capacity through their account team, with 30 days' notice. Increases can start sooner when capacity is available.\n\n## Billing\nThe new reservation is billed from the first full month after it starts. Reductions below the contracted minimum need a contract amendment.\n\n## Support\nSupport doesn't change reservations. Tell the customer their account team handles it.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-02-21._\n=== confluence/customer-success-and-support/known-issues-and-workarounds/embeddings-batch-usage-delay-2027.json\nKnown issue: Embeddings Batch usage can take up to 4 hours to appear | published | 2027-03-12\n## Scope\nThis applies only to the Embeddings Batch endpoint (`/v1/embeddings/batch`). Its usage is written when the whole batch finishes, so it can take up to 4 hours to appear on the Console usage page. It does not apply to Hosted API requests or ordinary embeddings calls; for those, the threshold is on the billing case routing page.\n\n## What to tell the customer\nBatch usage appears when the batch finishes. No case is needed within 4 hours of the batch finishing.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-12._\n=== confluence/customer-success-and-support/known-issues-and-workarounds/console-invoice-list-sort-order-2027.json\nKnown issue: Console invoice list sorts by issue date | published | 2027-03-05\n## Symptom\nThe Console invoice list sorts by the date an invoice was issued, not the period it covers, so a re-issued invoice for an earlier month can appear at the top.\n\n## Workaround\nUse the period filter or read the period on each invoice. Amounts and lines are correct.\n\n## Status\nFix planned for the Console release in May 2027.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-03-05._\n=== linear/engineering/ENG-9120-usage-pipeline-hourly-batch-cutover.json\nUsage pipeline: move the Console usage writer to hourly batches | Done | 2027-03-02\nSummary: The per-request usage writer cannot keep up with Q1 traffic: the Console usage page falls behind by hours during peaks and the backfill job double-writes on retry. Move the writer to hourly batches. Batched usage is complete and exact but appears in the Console up to 60 minutes after the request. Support's missing-usage threshold changes with the cut-over (Dev Patel to update the routing page). Plan: 1) Build the hourly batch writer behind a flag; write to a shadow table and compare totals for two weeks. 2) Freeze the per-request writer and switch the Console usage page to the batch table. 3) Cut over at a month boundary so no invoice period mixes both writers. 4) Remove the per-request writer and the backfill job. Acceptance: Shadow totals match the per-request writer to the token for 14 consecutive days. Every successful request appears on the Console usage page within 60 minutes. No invoice period contains usage from both writers.\ncomments ['Nadia Rahman: shadow totals matched for 16 days (2027-02-08 to 2027-02-23).', 'Logan Wright: cut-over scheduled for the March boundary, 2027-03-01 00:00 UTC.', 'Nadia Rahman: cut-over completed 2027-03-01 00:00 UTC. All requests made from then on are written by the hourly batch writer. Per-request writer frozen; backfill job removed.', 'Logan Wright: Dev confirmed the routing page change. Closing.']\n=== google_drive/shared_drives/customer-success/contract-account-credit-pilot.json\nPilot idea: let Support credit small duplicates on contract accounts (DRAFT) | draft | 2027-03-03\nDRAFT - not approved\n\nIdea: allow Support credit notes under $200 on contract accounts too, to skip the dispute step.\n\nBlocked on: the support charter forbids it today; needs a charter revision.\n=== google_drive/shared_drives/customer-success/missing-usage-threshold-proposal.json\nProposal: missing-usage threshold of 2 hours after the hourly batching (DRAFT) | draft | 2027-01-25\nDRAFT - not adopted\n\nWith hourly batching coming, should Support wait 2 hours before opening a Telemetry case for missing usage? This page is not about card payments that fail during a payment-processor incident; see the processor incidents page for those.\n\n## Collections\n- Billing Ops sends a reminder 5 days after the due date and retries an ACH pull on day 7.\n- Questions go to the account team.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-01-26._\n=== confluence/customer-success-and-support/support-process/academy-workshop-credit-codes-2027.json\nRedwood Academy Workshop Credit Codes (2027) | published | 2027-01-29\n## Purpose\nAttendees of Redwood Academy workshops get a one-time promotional code worth $100 of usage. This page tells Support what to say when someone asks about one.\n\n## What Support can do\n- Codes are issued by the Developer Relations team at the workshop. Support can't issue, re-send or extend them.\n- A code is redeemed in Console billing settings. Those are Market Development Funds (MDF), not billing credits.\n\n## What Support does\n- MDF requests are handled by Partnerships in the partner portal. Point the partner there; don't open a billing case.\n- MDF is paid against approved campaign invoices from the partner. Each line links to the jobs it bills.\n\n## Limits\n- Up to 50,000 requests per job.\n- Jobs that don't finish in 24 hours are cancelled; finished requests in a cancelled job are billed, unfinished ones are not.\n\n_Last reviewed 2027-01-20._\n=== confluence/product-docs/pricing-and-packaging/dedicated-reservation-changes-2027.json\nDedicated: Changing a Capacity Reservation (2027) | published | 2027-02-21\n## Changing a reservation\nDedicated customers change reserved capacity through their account team, with 30 days' notice. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EUBdNnPBsDk7kBNtF3wZtp","type":"tool_result","content":"published 2027-03-12\n## Scope\nIn scope:\n- Hosted API (usage-based)\n- Dedicated (reserved capacity contracts) when transacted via marketplace (where supported)\n- Private (platform fee + support) when transacted via marketplace (typically via private offers)\n- Compliance / support add-ons sold as separate dimensions/SKUs\n\nOut of scope:\n- Direct (non-marketplace) contracts and invoicing (see standard Order Form + invoicing runbook)\n- Technical metering implementation details (see Billing Catalog + Marketplace Metering docs)\n\n----\n## Definitions and key objects\n- Marketplace “Seller of Record”: AWS/GCP is the billing party to the end customer; Redwood receives net proceeds.\n- Marketplace “Private Offer”: negotiated pricing/terms within marketplace tooling; acceptance and billing occurs through AWS/GCP.\n- Entitlement: the marketplace subscription record used to provision/limit Redwood usage.\n- Metering dimension: the billable unit reported to AWS/GCP (e.g., tokens, requests, capacity-hours), mapped from Redwood internal meters.\n- Net proceeds: amount remitted to Redwood after marketplace revenue share and (where applicable) taxes handled by marketplace.\n\n----\n### Revenue recognition timing by purchase type\n1) Usage-based (Hosted API) via marketplace\n- Performance obligation: stand-ready access to API + actual usage (tokens/embeddings/reranks).\n- Recognition: as usage occurs (typically daily), based on Redwood internal metering as the system of record, reconciled to marketplace metering reports.\n- Cutoff: revenue accrued through period-end for delivered usage regardless of when marketplace remits cash.\n\n2) Committed spend / contract dimensions (where offered)\n- If structured as a committed spend with consumption over time:\n  - Recognize revenue as usage occurs up to the committed amount.\n  - Unused committed amount: generally deferred and recognized over the contractual period if stand-ready access is the performance obligation, subject to breakage assessment.\n- If the marketplace product is a fixed subscription for a term (e.g., “Platform fee per month”):\n  - Recognize ratably over the subscription term.\n\n3) Dedicated capacity reservations\n- Performance obligation: reserved capacity (stand-ready) and related platform access.\n- Recognition: ratably over the reservation term (monthly straight-line), unless the contract specifically ties c\n----\n### Multiple performance obligations and allocation\nIf an offer bundles multiple components (e.g., “Private Platform + Support + Compliance Add-on”), Finance must ensure:\n- Separate line items/dimensions exist where practical.\n- Standalone selling prices (SSP) are documented for allocation when components are bundled.\n\nAs a guardrail for marketplace packaging: avoid bundling implementation services into marketplace SKUs unless the marketplace supports clear separation and the legal terms align.\n\n----\n### Who invoices the customer\n- The marketplace invoices the customer. Redwood does not issue an invoice to the customer for marketplace-billed usage/subscriptions.\n- Redwood may provide a “billing support letter” or usage summary for customer procurement, but it must not be presented as a tax invoice.\n\n----\n### What we provide to customers\nPermitted (on request):\n- Usage reports (tokens, requests, model family, environment) from Redwood Console\n- Mapping of Redwood usage categories to marketplace dimensions (for customer chargeback)\n- Remittance timing expectations and support escalation contacts\n\nNot permitted (without Legal approval):\n- Re-issuing marketplace invoices with Redwood branding\n- Making commitments about marketplace tax treatment (VAT/GST) beyond directing customers to AWS/GCP invoice documentation\n\n----\n### Purchase Orders (POs) and vendor onboarding\n- Customers typically do vendor onboarding with AWS/GCP, not Redwood.\n- For Private Offers, customers may request Redwood W-9/W-8 or SOC documentation; coordinate via Security/Compliance and Legal, and clarify that billing is via marketplace.\n\n----\n## Refunds, credits, and adjustments\n----\n### General rule\n- Refunds/credits to marketplace customers must be processed through the marketplace mechanisms, not through Redwood’s direct invoicing.\n- Redwood may provide service credits inside Redwood (e.g., additional usage allowance) only if consistent with:\n  - Marketplace policies\n  - The customer’s private offer terms\n  - Revenue recognition guidance (credits may reduce transaction price)\n\n- Support: when a marketplace customer asks for a refund, a credit, or their invoice, point them to the marketplace's own process and invoice documentation. If they ask for a Redwood-branded invoice, tell them it would need Legal approval, which Redwood doesn't give for marketplace invoices. No Redwood case is needed for any of these; open a Billing Engineering case only for a Redwood vs marketplace metering mismatch.\n\n----\n### Usage-based disputes (Hosted API)\nCommon scenarios:\n- Customer claims unexpected spend due to misconfiguration\n- Metering mismatch (Redwood vs marketplace)\n\nProcess:\n1) Support confirms incident/dispute scope and time window.\n2) Billing Engineering validates internal metering and dimension mapping for the subscription entitlement.\n3) Finance determines whether an adjustment is warranted.\n4) If warranted:\n   - Prefer marketplace credit/refund path (AWS/GCP credit memo equivalent), following marketplace seller workflows.\n   - If marketplace cannot issue retroactive adjustments for certain dimensions, Legal + Finance decide on an alternative (e.g., issuing a future-period discount via private offer amendment).\n\nAccounting:\n- Refunds reduce revenue in the period identified (or as a subsequent event if applicable), with corresponding reduction in receivable from marketplace.\n\n----\n### Promotional credits / trials\nPolicy:\n- Avoid marketplace “$0 with credits” structures that are ambiguous for revrec.\n- If a marketplace trial is offered:\n  - Ensure the trial is clearly separate from paid subscription/usage.\n  - Track conversion and start date of paid entitlement.\n\nIf Redwood grants credits inside the Redwood platform (non-marketplace):\n- Treat as variable consideration / discount, and ensure it is reflected in net revenue where it impacts the transaction price.\n- Do not represent internal credits as marketplace credits.\n\n----\n### Source of truth\n- Redwood internal metering is the operational source of truth for usage delivered.\n- Marketplace reports are the commercial source of truth for amounts billed/collected.\n- Reconciliation is required at least monthly, and weekly during launch/transition periods.\n\n----\n### Recommended workflow\n1) Extract Redwood internal usage summary by marketplace entitlement\n- Group by: marketplace (AWS/GCP), seller account, entitlement ID, SKU, dimension, day/month\n\n2) Extract marketplace statements\n- AWS: metering/billing reports +
